**Jerusalem**[^cite_note-9] is a city in the [Southern Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Levant), on a plateau in the [Judaean Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaean_Mountains) between the [Mediterranean](19006.md) and the [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea). It is one of the [oldest cities in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_continuously_inhabited_cities) and is considered a [holy city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_city) to the three major [Abrahamic religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_religions): [Judaism](15624.md), [Christianity](5211.md) and [Islam](6037917.md). Both [Israel](9282173.md) and [Palestine](241405.md) claim Jerusalem as their [capital city](181337.md); Israel maintains its primary governmental institutions there, while Palestine ultimately foresees it as its seat of power. Neither claim is widely [recognised internationally](4024423.md).[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11]

At present, the [status of Jerusalem](4024423.md) remains one of the core issues in the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md). Under the 1947 [United Nations Partition Plan for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine), Jerusalem was to be "established as a *[corpus separatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corpus_separatum_\(Jerusalem\))* under a special international regime" administered by the United Nations.[^cite_note-\:0-12] Following the [1948 Arab–Israeli War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War), an [armistice agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1949_Armistice_Agreements) were reached in 1949 resulting in the division of Jerusalem into Israeli-controlled West Jerusalem and Jordanian-controlled East Jerusalem. This agreement remained until 1967, when Israel occupied East Jerusalem from Jordan following the [Six-Day War](29328.md) and subsequently annexed it into the city's municipality, together with additional surrounding territory.[^cite_note-16] One of Israel's [Basic Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Laws_of_Israel), the 1980 [Jerusalem Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Law), refers to Jerusalem as the country's undivided capital. All branches of the Israeli government are located in Jerusalem, including the [Knesset](74656.md) (Israel's parliament), the [residences of the prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Aghion) and [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_HaNassi), and the [Supreme Court](1666510.md).

Throughout [its long history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Jerusalem), Jerusalem has been destroyed at least twice, [besieged 23 times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Jerusalem_\(disambiguation\)), captured and recaptured 44 times, and attacked 52 times.[^cite_note-17] The part of Jerusalem called the [City of David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_David_\(archaeological_site\)) shows first signs of settlement in the 4th millennium BCE, in the shape of encampments of nomadic shepherds.[^cite_note-18] During the [Canaanite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaan#Canaanites) period (14th century BCE) Jerusalem was named as *Urusalim* on [ancient Egyptian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Egypt) tablets, probably meaning "foundation of \[the god] [Shalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalim)" after a [Canaanite deity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaanite_religion). [Radiocarbon dating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiocarbon_dating) of 103 samples from Iron Age Jerusalem provides evidence for settlement in the 12th–10th centuries BCE, westward expansion and extensive construction by the 9th century BCE, and subsequent fortification and growth until the Babylonian destruction in 586 BCE.[^cite_note-19] In 1538 the [city walls were rebuilt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walls_of_Jerusalem) for a last time around Jerusalem under [Suleiman the Magnificent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suleiman_the_Magnificent) of the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md). Today those walls define the [Old City](302972.md), which since the 19th century has been divided into four quarters—the [Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Quarter), [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Quarter), [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Quarter_\(Jerusalem\)) and [Muslim quarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Quarter_\(Jerusalem\)).[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-Teller-2022-21] The Old City became a [World Heritage Site](44940.md) in 1981, and is on the [List of World Heritage in Danger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_in_Danger).[^cite_note-22] Since 1860 [Jerusalem has grown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expansion_of_Jerusalem_in_the_19th_century) far beyond the Old City's boundaries. In 2024 Jerusalem had a [population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Jerusalem) of 1,050,153.[^cite_note-Israelpopulations-5] In 2022 60% were Jews and almost 40% were Palestinians.[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-25] In 2020 the population was 951,100, of which Jews comprised 570,100 (59.9%), Muslims 353,800 (37.2%), Christians 16,300 (1.7%) and 10,800 unclassified (1.1%).[^cite_note-jerusaleminstitute.org.il-2022-26]

According to the [Hebrew Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanakh), King [David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David) [conquered the city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Jebus) from the [Jebusites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jebusite) and established it as the capital of the [United Kingdom of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Israel_\(united_monarchy\)), and his son King [Solomon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon) commissioned the building of the [First Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_in_Jerusalem).[^cite_note-bible-david-28] Modern scholars argue that Israelites branched out of the [Canaanite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaan) peoples and culture and developed a distinct [monolatrous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monolatry)—and later [monotheistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monotheism)—religion centred on [El](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_\(deity\))/[Yahweh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahweh).[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30] These foundational events assumed central symbolic [importance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_in_Judaism) for the Jewish people.[^cite_note-Since_the_10th_century_BCE-31][^cite_note-32] The [sobriquet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sobriquet) of holy city (Hebrew: עיר הקודש, romanised: *'Ir ha-Qodesh*) was probably attached to Jerusalem in [post-exilic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-exilic) times.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35] The holiness of [Jerusalem in Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_in_Christianity), conserved in the [Greek translation of the Hebrew Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septuagint),[^cite_note-36] which Christians adopted as the [Old Testament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Testament),[^cite_note-37] was reinforced by the [New Testament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Testament) account of [Jesus's crucifixion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crucifixion_of_Jesus) and [resurrection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resurrection_of_Jesus) there. In Islam, Jerusalem is the third-holiest city, after [Mecca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecca) and [Medina](36636.md).[^cite_note-Esposito-2002-38][^cite_note-39] The city was the first [standard direction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qibla) for [Muslim prayers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salah),[^cite_note-40] and [in Islamic tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiest_sites_in_Islam), [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad) made his [Night Journey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isra_and_Mi'raj) there in 621, ascending to heaven where he spoke to [God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/God_in_Islam), per the [Quran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran).[^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42] As a result, despite having an area of only 0.9 km<sup>2</sup> (3⁄8 sq mi),[^cite_note-43] the Old City is home to many sites of seminal [religious importance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_significance_of_Jerusalem), among them the [Al-Aqsa Mosque Compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa) on the [Temple Mount](65719.md) with its [Western Wall](75225.md), [Dome of the Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dome_of_the_Rock) and [Qibli (al-Aqsa) Mosque](65759.md), and the [Church of the Holy Sepulchre](7810.md).

## Etymology and names

Further information: [Names of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_Jerusalem)

### Etymology

The name *Jerusalem* is variously etymologised to mean 'foundation of the god [Shalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shalim)',[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-Binz-2005-45] from the Semitic root *yry* which can mean 'to found, to lay a cornerstone'[^cite_note-46] and is used that way at least twice in the Hebrew Bible, in [Job](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Job)[^cite_note-47] and [Genesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Genesis).[^cite_note-48] The god Shalem was thus the original [tutelary deity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutelary_deity) of the Bronze Age city.[^cite_note-49]

Shalim or Shalem was the name of the god of dusk in the [Canaanite religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaanite_religion), whose name is based on the same root [S-L-M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-L-M) from which the Hebrew word for "peace" is derived (*Shalom* in [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language), cognate with [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Arabic) *Salam*).[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] The name thus offered itself to etymologisations such as "The City of Peace",[^cite_note-Binz-2005-45][^cite_note-52] "Abode of Peace",[^cite_note-Bosworth-2007-53][^cite_note-Denise_DeGarmo-2011-54] "Dwelling of Peace" ("founded in safety"),[^cite_note-55] or "Vision of Peace" in some Christian authors.[^cite_note-56]

The ending *-ayim* indicates the [dual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_\(grammatical_number\)), thus leading to the suggestion that the name *Yerushalayim* refers to the fact that the city initially sat on two hills.[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58]

### Ancient Egyptian sources

The [Execration Texts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Execration_Texts) of the [Middle Kingdom of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Kingdom_of_Egypt) (c. 19th century BCE), which refer to a city called *rwšꜣlmm* or *[ꜣwšꜣmm](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/%EA%9C%A3w%C5%A1%EA%9C%A3mm)*, variously transcribed as *Rušalimum*, or *Urušalimum*,[^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60] may indicate Jerusalem.[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62] Alternatively, the [Amarna letters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amarna_letters) of [Abdi-Heba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdi-Heba) (1330s BCE), which reference an *Úrušalim*, may be the earliest mention of the city.[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65]

### Hebrew Bible and Jewish sources

The form *Yerushalem* ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): ירושלם) or *Yerushalayim* ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): ירושלים) first appears in the Bible, in the [Book of Joshua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Joshua).[^cite_note-Zondervan-66] Jerusalem is mentioned 669 times in the [Hebrew Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible), while the term [Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zion) appears 154 times as either a symbolic mention of Jerusalem or as a reference to the entire [Land of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_of_Israel).[^cite_note-67]

According to a [Midrash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midrash), the name is a combination of two names united by God, *Yireh* ("the abiding place", the name given by [Abraham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham) to the place where [he planned to sacrifice his son](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binding_of_Isaac)) and *[Shalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_\(Bible\))* ("Place of Peace", the name given by high priest [Shem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shem)).[^cite_note-68] [2 Chronicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2_Chronicles) ascribes Mount Moriah, the traditional location of the Binding of Isaac, as the site where King [Solomon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon) built the [First Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon's_Temple).[^cite_note-Zondervan-66]

### Oldest written mention of *Jerusalem*

One of the earliest extra-biblical [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language) writing of the word *Jerusalem* is dated to the sixth or seventh century BCE[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70] and was discovered in [Khirbet Beit Lei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khirbet_Beit_Lei) near [Beit Guvrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Guvrin,_Israel) in 1961. The [inscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khirbet_Beit_Lei_graffiti) states: "I am Yahweh thy God, I will accept the cities of Judah and I will redeem Jerusalem",[^cite_note-71][^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] or as other scholars suggest: "Yahweh is the God of the whole earth. The mountains of Judah belong to him, to the God of Jerusalem".[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] An earlier example of the name appears in a papyrus from the 7th century BCE.[^cite_note-76][^cite_note-Reich,_Ronny-2020-77]



Close up of the [Khirbet Beit Lei inscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khirbet_Beit_Lei_graffiti), showing the earliest extra-biblical Hebrew writing of the word *Jerusalem*, dated to the seventh or sixth century BCE

A papyrus fragment from the Judean Desert that has been interpreted as reading "from the king’s maidservant, from Naharata, jars of wine, to Jerusalem" was dated approximately to the seventh century BCE using a combination of [palaeographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palaeography) analysis of the script and [radiocarbon dating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiocarbon_dating).[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79]

In extra-biblical inscriptions, the earliest known example of the *-ayim* ending was discovered on a column about 3 km west of ancient Jerusalem, dated to the first century BCE.[^cite_note-Reich,_Ronny-2020-77]

### Jebus, Zion, City of David

An ancient settlement of Jerusalem, founded as early as the [Bronze Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronze_Age) on the hill above the [Gihon Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gihon_Spring), was, according to the Bible, named [Jebus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jebusite).[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82] Called the "Fortress of Zion" (*metsudat Zion*), it was renamed as the "City of David",[^cite_note-83] and was known by this name in antiquity.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85] Another name, "[Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zion)", initially referred to a distinct part of the city, but later came to signify the city as a whole, and afterwards to represent the whole biblical [Land of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_of_Israel).

### Greek, Roman and Byzantine names

In Greek and Latin the city's name was transliterated *Hierosolyma/Hierosoluma* (Greek: Ἱεροσόλυμα; in Greek *hieròs*, *ἱερός*, means holy), and was the term used by [Matthew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_the_Apostle) and [Mark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_the_Evangelist) in their [gospels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel) instead of the Hebrew term.[^cite_note-Brenk-2011-86]

Up until the 2010s the consensus among historians was that following [Alexander the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_the_Great)'s conquest, Hierosoluma was set to be incorporated into the larger temple cities of the [Seleucid kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seleucid_kingdom), and to be [Hellenised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenised) as *Hierapolis.*[^cite_note-Brenk-2011-86] However, modern historians dispute this as a proper Ancient Greek translation for the [polis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polis) would be similar to *Hierolophos*.[^cite_note-Brenk-2011-86]

After the [Bar Kokhba revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_Kokhba_revolt) (132–136 CE), the city was renamed [Aelia Capitolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aelia_Capitolina) during the [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome) period of its history. Named for the Roman emperor [Hadrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadrian) and the deities of Rome, the name lasted until the Byzantine era.[^cite_note-87]

### Salem

The Biblical city "Salem" (שלם), said to be the kingdom of [Melchizedek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melchizedek) in Genesis 14,[^cite_note-88] has traditionally been identified with Jerusalem, reflecting an early name of the city.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] [Josephus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josephus),[^cite_note-91] the early Aramaic translations of the relevant verse,[^cite_note-92] as well as the [Aramaic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aramaic_language) [Apocryphon of Genesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesis_Apocryphon) of the [Dead Sea Scrolls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea_Scrolls) (1QapGen 22\:13), all make this identification. Salem as an early name for Jerusalem is also indicated by the liturgical poetry in [Psalms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psalms), which equates Salem with [Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zion), used as a synonym for Jerusalem.[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94]

Other early Hebrew sources,[^cite_note-95] early Christian renderings of the verse[^cite_note-96] and *[targumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targumim)*,[^cite_note-97] however, put Salem in Northern Israel near [Shechem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shechem) (Sichem), now [Nablus](233369.md), a city of some importance in early sacred Hebrew writing.[^cite_note-98] Possibly the redactor of the Apocryphon of Genesis wanted to dissociate Melchizedek from the area of Shechem, which at the time was in possession of the [Samaritans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samaritans).[^cite_note-99] However that may be, later Rabbinic sources also equate Salem with Jerusalem, mainly to link Melchizedek to later Temple traditions.[^cite_note-100] Samaritan tradition, which consencrated [Mount Gerizim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Gerizim) over Jerusalem, referred to the later as *Cursed Salem* (Arur-Shalem ארור שלם).[^cite_note-101]

### Arabic names

"Al-Quds" redirects here. For other uses, see [Al-Quds (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_\(disambiguation\)).

Originally titled Bayt al-Maqdis, today, Jerusalem is most commonly known in [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic) as القُدس, transliterated as *al-Quds* and meaning "the holy" or "the holy sanctuary",[^cite_note-Bosworth-2007-53][^cite_note-Denise_DeGarmo-2011-54] cognate with [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): הקדש, [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Hebrew): *ha-qodesh*. The name is possibly a shortened form of مدينة القُدس *Madīnat al-Quds* "city of the holy sanctuary" after the Hebrew nickname with the same meaning, *Ir ha-Qodesh* (עיר הקדש). The ق (Q) is pronounced either with a [voiceless uvular plosive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voiceless_uvular_plosive) (/q/), as in [Classical Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_Arabic), or with a [glottal stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glottal_stop) (ʔ) as in [Levantine Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levantine_Arabic).[^cite_note-Elihay-2011-8] Official Israeli government policy mandates that أُورُشَلِيمَ, transliterated as *Ūrušalīm*, which is the name frequently used in Christian translations of the Bible into Arabic,[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103] be used as the Arabic language name for the city in conjunction with القُدس, giving أُورُشَلِيمَ-القُدس, Ūrušalīm-al-Quds.[^cite_note-104] Palestinian Arab families who hail from this city are often called "*Qudsi*" (قُدسي) or "*Maqdasi*" (مقدسي), while Palestinian Muslim Jerusalemites may use these terms as a [demonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demonym).[^cite_note-105]

## History

Main article: [History of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Jerusalem)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Jerusalem).

See also: [Historical maps of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartography_of_Jerusalem)

| icon | This section **does not [cite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Citing_sources) any [sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability)**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Jerusalem) by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Referencing_for_beginners). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence). *(October 2026)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

Jerusalem is one of the world's oldest cities, with a history spanning over 5,000 years. Its origins trace back to around 3000 BCE, with the first settlement near the [Gihon Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gihon_Spring). The city is first mentioned in Egyptian [Execration texts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Execration_texts) around 2000 BCE as "Rusalimum." By the 17th century BCE Jerusalem had developed into a fortified city under [Canaanite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaan) rule, with massive walls protecting its water system. During the [Late Bronze Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Bronze_Age) Jerusalem became a vassal of [Ancient Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Egypt), as documented in the [Amarna letters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amarna_letters).

The city's importance grew during the *Israelite period*, which began around 1000 BCE when King [David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David) captured Jerusalem and made it the capital of the [United Kingdom of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Israel). David's son King [Solomon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon) built the [First Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Temple), establishing the city as a major religious centre. Following the kingdom's split, Jerusalem became the capital of the [Kingdom of Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Judah) until it was captured by the [Neo-Babylonian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Babylonian_Empire) in 586 BCE. The [Babylonians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylonians) destroyed the First Temple, leading to the [Babylonian exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylonian_exile) of the Jewish population. After the [Persian conquest of Babylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Babylon) in 539 BCE, [Cyrus the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrus_the_Great) allowed the Jews to return from exile and rebuild the city and its temple, whose construction began c. 516 BCE, marking the start of the [Second Temple period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Temple_period).[^cite_note-106] Jerusalem fell under [Hellenistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenistic) rule after the conquests of [Alexander the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_the_Great) in 332 BCE, leading to increasing cultural and political influence from [Greece](12108.md). The [Hasmonean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasmonean) revolt in 164 BCE briefly restored Jewish sovereignty, with Jerusalem as the capital of an independent state.

In 63 BCE, Jerusalem [was captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Jerusalem_\(63_BC\)) by [Pompey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pompey) and brought under the rule of the [Roman Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Republic). The city was embellished by [Herod the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herod_the_Great), who erected a series of supporting walls that allowed for the enlargement of the Temple Mount and the expansion of the Second Temple,[^cite_note-107] making it one of the largest sanctuaries in the [ancient world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_world). Tensions between the Jews and the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) eventually escalated into the [First Jewish Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Jewish%E2%80%93Roman_War), resulting in the [siege and destruction of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Jerusalem_\(70_CE\)) and the [Second Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Temple) in 70 CE. A few decades later, the city was rebuilt as the Roman colony *[Aelia Capitolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aelia_Capitolina)*, dedicated to [Jupiter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jupiter_\(god\)), provoking the [Bar Kokhba Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_Kokhba_revolt) (132–136 CE). After the revolt's suppression, Jews were banned from the city. During the [Byzantine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Palestine) period, Jerusalem gained prominence as a centre of [Christian](5211.md) pilgrimage, especially after [Constantine the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantine_the_Great) supported the construction of the [Church of the Holy Sepulchre](7810.md). In 638 CE, Jerusalem was conquered by the [Rashidun Caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashidun_Caliphate), and under early [Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic) rule, the [Dome of the Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dome_of_the_Rock) and [Qibli (al-Aqsa) Mosque](65759.md) were built, solidifying its religious importance in Islam.

During the [Crusades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusades) Jerusalem changed hands multiple times, being captured by the Crusaders in 1099 and recaptured by [Saladin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saladin) in 1187. It remained under [Islamic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic) control through the [Ayyubid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayyubid) and [Mamluk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamluk_Sultanate) periods, until it became part of the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md) in 1517. In the modern period Jerusalem was divided between Israel and Jordan after the [1948 Arab–Israeli War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War). Israel captured [East Jerusalem](343508.md) during the [Six-Day War](29328.md) in 1967, uniting the city under Israeli control. The [status of Jerusalem](4024423.md) remains a highly contentious issue, with both Israelis and Palestinians claiming it as their capital. Historiographically, the city's history is often interpreted through the lens of competing national narratives. [Israeli](181296.md) scholars emphasise the ancient Jewish connection to the city, while [Palestinian](23267.md) narratives highlight the city's broader historical and multicultural significance. Both perspectives influence contemporary discussions of Jerusalem's status and future.

## Political status

Main article: [Positions on Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Positions_on_Jerusalem)



An Israeli stamp from 1968, quoting\
[Psalm 122](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psalm_122)\:6;\
*Pray for the peace of Jerusalem!...*

From 1923 until 1948 Jerusalem served as the administrative capital of [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine).[^cite_note-108]

In December 1949, Israeli prime minister [David Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion) declared the area of [West Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Jerusalem) that it controlled as "Israel's eternal capital,"[^cite_note-109] a policy that was affirmed by the [Knesset](74656.md) in January 1950.[^cite_note-110] This policy was not recognised as such internationally because [UN General Assembly Resolution 194](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_194) envisaged Jerusalem as an [international city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_city); the American government opposed the move and insisted that Israel's capital remain in Tel Aviv.[^cite_note-111] As a result of the Six-Day War in 1967, the whole of Jerusalem [came under Israeli control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reunification_of_Jerusalem). On 27 June 1967, the government of [Levi Eshkol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi_Eshkol) extended Israeli law and jurisdiction to East Jerusalem, but agreed that administration of the Temple Mount compound would be maintained by the [Jordanian waqf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Islamic_Waqf), under the Jordanian Ministry of Religious Endowments.[^cite_note-112]

In 1988, the Israeli government ordered the closure of [Orient House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orient_House), home of the Arab Studies Society and headquarters of the [Palestine Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization), for security reasons. The building reopened in 1992 as a Palestinian guesthouse.[^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114] The [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords) stated that the final status of Jerusalem would be determined by negotiations with the [Palestinian Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_National_Authority). The accords banned any official Palestinian presence in the city until a final peace agreement, but provided for the opening of a Palestinian trade office in East Jerusalem. The Palestinian Authority regards East Jerusalem as the capital of a future Palestinian state.[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116]

President [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md) has said that any agreement that did not include East Jerusalem as the capital of Palestine would be unacceptable.[^cite_note-117] Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu has similarly stated that Jerusalem would remain the undivided capital of Israel. Due to its proximity to the city, especially the Temple Mount, [Abu Dis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dis), a Palestinian suburb of Jerusalem, has been proposed as the future capital of a Palestinian state by Israel. Israel has not incorporated Abu Dis within its security wall around Jerusalem. The Palestinian Authority has built a possible future parliament building for the [Palestinian Legislative Council](40067464.md) in the town, and its Jerusalem Affairs Offices are all located in Abu Dis.[^cite_note-118]

### International status

Most of the international community regards East Jerusalem, including the entire Old City, as part of the [occupied Palestinian territories](241405.md). The United States recognized Israeli control over all of Jerusalem in 2017.[^cite_note-119] Until then neither part, West or East Jerusalem, was recognized as part of the territory of the [State of Israel](9282173.md) or the [State of Palestine](241405.md).[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] The move was followed by additional states to recognizing West Jerusalem as part of Israel or East Jerusalem as part of a future State of Palestine.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126][^cite_note-127] Under the [United Nations Partition Plan for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine) adopted by the [General Assembly of the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Assembly_of_the_United_Nations) in 1947, Jerusalem was envisaged to become a [corpus separatum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corpus_separatum_\(Jerusalem\)) administered by the United Nations for ten years, following which city residents would be able to "express by means of a referendum their wishes."[^cite_note-\:0-12] In the war of 1948 the western part of the city was occupied by forces of the nascent state of Israel, while the eastern part was occupied by Jordan. The international community largely considers the legal status of Jerusalem to derive from the partition plan, and correspondingly most refuse to recognise Israeli sovereignty over the city.[^cite_note-128]

### Status under Israeli rule

Following the 1967 Six-Day War, Israel extended its jurisdiction and administration over East Jerusalem, establishing new municipal borders.



The [Knesset](74656.md) houses the [legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislature) of Israel

In 2010 Israel approved legislation giving Jerusalem the highest national priority status in Israel. The law prioritised construction throughout the city, and offered grants and tax benefits to residents to make housing, infrastructure, education, employment, business, tourism and cultural events more affordable. Communications Minister [Moshe Kahlon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Kahlon) said that the bill sent "a clear, unequivocal political message that Jerusalem will not be divided", and that "all those within the Palestinian and international community who expect the current Israeli government to accept any demands regarding Israel's sovereignty over its capital are mistaken and misleading".[^cite_note-129]

The status of the city, and especially its holy places, remains a core issue in the Israeli–Palestinian conflict. The Israeli government has approved building plans in the Muslim Quarter of the Old City[^cite_note-130] in order to expand the Jewish presence in East Jerusalem, while some Islamic leaders have made claims that Jews have no historical connection to Jerusalem, alleging that the 2,500-year-old Western Wall was constructed as part of a mosque.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] Palestinians regard Jerusalem as the capital of the [State of Palestine](241405.md),[^cite_note-In_the_Palestine_Liberation_Organization-133] and the city's borders have been the subject of bilateral talks. A team of experts assembled by the then Israeli Prime Minister [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak) in 2000 concluded that the city must be divided, since Israel had failed to achieve any of its national aims there.[^cite_note-134]

However, Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) said in 2014 that "Jerusalem will never be divided".[^cite_note-135] A poll conducted in June 2013 found that 74% of Israeli Jews reject the idea of a Palestinian capital in any portion of Jerusalem, though 72% of the public regarded it as a divided city.[^cite_note-136] A poll conducted by the Palestinian Centre for Public Opinion and American Pechter Middle East Polls for the [Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_on_Foreign_Relations) among East Jerusalem Arab residents in 2011 revealed that 39% of East Jerusalem Arab residents would prefer Israeli citizenship, while 31% opted for Palestinian citizenship. According to the poll 40% of Palestinian residents would prefer to leave their neighbourhoods if they would be placed under Palestinian rule.[^cite_note-137]



The [Supreme Court of Israel](1666510.md)

### Jerusalem as capital of Israel

Main article: [Status of Jerusalem](4024423.md)



The [Israeli Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Ministry_of_Israel) building

On 5 December 1949 Israel's first prime minister, [David Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion), proclaimed Jerusalem as Israel's "eternal" and "sacred" capital, and eight days later specified that only the war had "compelled" the Israeli leadership "to establish the seat of Government in Tel Aviv", while "for the State of Israel there has always been and always will be one capital only—Jerusalem the Eternal", and that after the war, efforts had been ongoing for creating the conditions for "the Knesset... returning to Jerusalem."[^cite_note-138] This indeed took place, and since the beginning of 1950 all branches of the [Israeli government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Israel)—[legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Israel#Legislative_branch), [judicial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Israel#Judicial_system) and [executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Israel#Prime_Ministers_and_governments_in_the_last_ten_years)—have resided there, except for the [Ministry of Defense](3226446.md), which is located at [HaKirya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HaKirya) in [Tel Aviv](31453.md).[^cite_note-139][^cite_note-Knesset_Proclaims_Jerusalem_As_Israel-1950-140] At the time of Ben Gurion's proclamations and the ensuing Knesset vote of 24 January 1950,[^cite_note-Knesset_Proclaims_Jerusalem_As_Israel-1950-140] Jerusalem was divided between Israel and Jordan, and thus the proclamation only applied to West Jerusalem.

In July 1980 Israel passed the [Jerusalem Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Law) as [Basic Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Laws_of_Israel). The law declared Jerusalem the "complete and united" capital of Israel.[^cite_note-141] The Jerusalem Law was condemned by the international community, which did not recognise Jerusalem as the capital of Israel. The United Nations Security Council passed [Resolution 478](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_478) on 20 August 1980, which declared that the Jerusalem Law is *"a violation of international law"*, is *"null and void and must be rescinded forthwith"*. Member states were called upon to withdraw their diplomatic representation from Jerusalem.[^cite_note-142]

Following the resolution, 22 of the 24 countries that previously had their embassy in (West) Jerusalem relocated them in Tel Aviv, where many embassies already resided prior to Resolution 478. [Costa Rica](5551.md) and [El Salvador](9356.md) followed in 2006.[^cite_note-143] There are five embassies—United States, Guatemala, Honduras, Papua New Guinea and Kosovo—and two consulates located within the city limits of Jerusalem. Paraguay maintains an embassy in the [Jerusalem District](844333.md) [town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_council_\(Israel\)) of [Mevaseret Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevaseret_Zion),[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] in addition to Bolivia whose embassy is now [closed](58066729.md#2023:_Bolivia_severs_relations_with_Israel).[^cite_note-148] There are [a number of consulates-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Consulates-General_in_Jerusalem) located in Jerusalem, which work primarily either with Israel, or the Palestinian authorities.

In 1995 the [United States Congress](31756.md) passed the [Jerusalem Embassy Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Embassy_Act), which required, subject to conditions, that its embassy be moved from Tel Aviv to Jerusalem.[^cite_note-149] On 6 December 2017 the [US president](24113.md), [Donald Trump](4848272.md), officially [recognised Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_recognition_of_Jerusalem_as_capital_of_Israel) as Israel's capital and announced his intention to move the [American embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Tel_Aviv) to Jerusalem, reversing decades of United States policy on the issue.[^cite_note-150][^cite_note-151] The move was criticised by many nations.[^cite_note-152] A resolution condemning the US decision was supported by all the 14 other members of the UN Security Council, but was vetoed by the US on 18 December 2017.[^cite_note-153] A subsequent resolution condemning the US decision was passed in the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md).[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] On 14 May 2018 the United States officially opened its [embassy in Jerusalem](57417950.md), transforming its Tel Aviv location into a consulate. Due to the general lack of international recognition of Jerusalem as Israel's capital, some non-Israeli media outlets use Tel Aviv as a [metonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metonym) for Israel.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160][^cite_note-161]

In December 2017, US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) made the decision to acknowledge Jerusalem as the capital of Israel and relocate the US embassy from [Tel Aviv](31453.md) to that city.[^cite_note-162] This decision faced significant opposition, particularly from leaders within the Arab and Muslim communities. The Palestinians assert that East Jerusalem should serve as the capital of their future state, and its status ought to be resolved through peace negotiations as outlined in the 1993 Israeli-Palestinian peace accords. Palestinian President [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md) remarked that Trump's action effectively negated the United States' position as a mediator in the peace process. [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md), the head of the Islamist Hamas movement, urged for a new "intifada," or uprising. Turkish President [Recep Tayyip Erdogan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) criticised the decision, stating it would "place the region in a ring of fire." Saudi Arabia's [King Salman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salman_of_Saudi_Arabia) described it as "a clear provocation to Muslims globally." Egyptian President [Abdel Fattah al-Sisi](36703624.md) indicated that this decision would exacerbate the situation in the region. [Iran](14653.md) warned that the decision would incite a "new intifada," labelling it a blatant infringement of international resolutions.[^cite_note-163]

In April 2017 the Russian [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](4432213.md) announced it viewed Western Jerusalem as Israel's capital in the context of UN-approved principles which include the status of East Jerusalem as the capital of the future Palestinian state.[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166] On 15 December 2018 the Australian government officially recognised West Jerusalem as Israel's capital, but said their embassy in Tel Aviv would stay until a two-state resolution was settled.[^cite_note-167] The decision was reversed in October 2022.[^cite_note-168]

#### Government precinct and national institutions

The [Kiryat HaLeom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_HaLeom) (national precinct) project is intended to house most government agencies and national cultural institutions. They are located in the [Kiryat HaMemshala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_HaMemshala) (government complex) in the [Givat Ram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Givat_Ram) neighbourhood. Some government buildings are located in [Kiryat Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Menachem_Begin). The city is home to the Knesset,[^cite_note-169] the [Supreme Court](1666510.md),[^cite_note-170] the [Bank of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Israel), the [National Headquarters of the Israel Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Headquarters_of_the_Israel_Police), the official residences of the [president](153049.md) and the [prime minister](24898.md), the [Cabinet](5006115.md), and all ministries except for the [Ministry of Defense](3226446.md) (which is located in central Tel Aviv's [HaKirya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HaKirya) district) and the [Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Agriculture_and_Rural_Development_\(Israel\)) (which is located in [Rishon LeZion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishon_LeZion), in the wider Tel Aviv [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_area), near [Beit Dagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Dagan)).

#### Israeli settlements

See also: [Israeli settlements](15123.md)

You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Jerusalem\&action=edit\&section=)[adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Jerusalem\&action=edit\&section=) or [making an edit request](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Talk%3AJerusalem\&preload=Template%3ASubmit+an+edit+request%2Fpreload\&action=edit\&section=new\&editintro=Template%3AEdit+extended-protected%2Feditintro\&preloadtitle=Extended-confirmed-protected+edit+request+on+3+October+2026\&preloadparams%5B%5D=edit+extended-protected\&preloadparams%5B%5D=Jerusalem). *(April 2023)* |
| --- | --- |

Since its capture in 1967, the Israeli government has built 12 [Israeli settlements](15123.md) in [East Jerusalem](343508.md), with a population amounting to 220,000 Israeli Jewish settlers as of 2019.[^cite_note-171] The international community consider Israeli settlements to be illegal under [international law](8195726.md).[^cite_note-172]

### Jerusalem as capital of Palestine

See also: [East Jerusalem § Jerusalem as capital](343508.md#Jerusalem_as_capital)



The Orient House, Jerusalem the former headquarters of the PLO



The [Consulate General of France, Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consulate_General_of_France,_Jerusalem)

The [Palestinian National Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_National_Authority) views East Jerusalem as [occupied territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupied_territory) according to [United Nations Security Council Resolution 242](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_242). The Palestinian Authority claims Jerusalem, including the [Al-Aqsa Mosque Compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa), as the capital of the State of Palestine,[^cite_note-In_the_Palestine_Liberation_Organization-133] The PLO claims that West Jerusalem is also subject to permanent status negotiations. However, it has stated that it would be willing to consider alternative solutions, such as making Jerusalem an [open city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_city).[^cite_note-173]

The PLO's position is that East Jerusalem, as defined by the [pre-1967 municipal boundaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)), shall be the capital of Palestine and [West Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Jerusalem) the capital of Israel, with each state enjoying full sovereignty over its respective part of the city and with its own municipality. A joint *development council* would be responsible for coordinated development.[^cite_note-174] [Orient House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orient_House) in East Jerusalem served as the headquarters of the [PLO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PLO) in the 1980s and 1990s. It was closed by Israel in 2001, two days after the [Sbarro restaurant suicide bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sbarro_restaurant_suicide_bombing).

Some states, such as Russia[^cite_note-175] and China,[^cite_note-176] recognise the Palestinian state with East Jerusalem as its capital. [United Nations General Assembly Resolution 58/292](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_58/292) affirmed that the Palestinian people have the right to sovereignty over East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-177]

#### Palestinian offices and institutions

See also: [List of diplomatic missions in Palestine § Consulates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_Palestine#Consulates)



[Villa Salameh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villa_Salameh)—the home of the Belgian Consulate to Palestine

Government offices are located outside the Israeli municipal limits include the [Palestinian Security Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Security_Services), [Force 17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Force_17), the [Preventative Security Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preventative_Security_Service) and the [Ministry of Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_\(State_of_Palestine\)).[^cite_note-178][^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] There is a Palestinian Authority regional office and an electoral office located in the [Dahiyat al Barid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ram) neighbourhood.[^cite_note-181]

## Municipal administration

Main article: [Municipality of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipality_of_Jerusalem)

The Jerusalem [City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_council) is a body of 31 elected members headed by the mayor, who serves a five-year term and appoints eight deputies. The former mayor of Jerusalem, [Uri Lupolianski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uri_Lupolianski), was elected in 2003.[^cite_note-Cidor-2007-182] In the November 2008 city elections, [Nir Barkat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nir_Barkat) was elected. In November 2018 [Moshe Lion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Lion) was elected mayor.[^cite_note-183]

Apart from the mayor and his deputies, City Council members receive no salaries and work on a voluntary basis. The longest-serving Jerusalem mayor is [Teddy Kollek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teddy_Kollek), who spent 28 years—six consecutive terms—in office. Most of the meetings of the Jerusalem City Council are private, but each month, it holds a session that is open to the public.[^cite_note-Cidor-2007-182] Within the city council, religious political parties form an especially powerful faction, accounting for the majority of its seats.[^cite_note-184]

The headquarters of the Jerusalem Municipality and the mayor's office are at [Safra Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safra_Square) (*Kikar Safra*) on [Jaffa Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffa_Road). The municipal complex, comprising two modern buildings and ten renovated historic buildings surrounding a large plaza, opened in 1993 when it moved from the [old town hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Old_Town_Hall) building built by the [Mandate authorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandate_Palestine).[^cite_note-185] The city falls under the [Jerusalem District](844333.md), with Jerusalem as the district's capital. 37% of the population is Palestinian, but in 2014 not more than 10% of tax revenues were allocated for them. In East Jerusalem, 52% of the land was excluded from development, 35% designated for Jewish settlements, and 13% for Palestinian use, almost all of which was already built upon.[^cite_note-186]

In the [Oslo I Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_I_Accord), certain parts of few neighbourhoods were allotted to the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md). Parts of [Sur Baher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sur_Baher), Wadi al-Hummus, [Umm Leisun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umm_Leisun_inscription) and [Umm Tuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umm_Tuba), altogether came under [Area A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_A), which is completely controlled by the Palestinian Authority.[^cite_note-187] [Al-Ram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ram) and Dahiyat al-Barid are mostly in [Area B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_B), where both Palestine and Israel has control.[^cite_note-188] Other parts of [Beit Hanina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanina), [Kafr Aqab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafr_Aqab) and [Arab al-Jahalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/'Arab_al-Jahalin) also falls under Area B.[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191]

### Voting Patterns

In the [2022 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Israeli_legislative_election), 75 percent of voters in Jerusalem voted for the parties of the right-wing coalition led by [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), while 20 percent voted for the parties of the center-left coalition led by [Yair Lapid](2448072.md) and 2 percent voted for the Arab parties. 42 percent of voters voted for the [Haredi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haredi) parties [Shas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shas) or [UTJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Torah_Judaism).[^cite_note-192]

## Geography



A panorama of the [Temple Mount](65719.md) (Haram al-Sharif or Al-Aqsa compound), including [Al-Aqsa Mosque](65759.md), and [Dome of the Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dome_of_the_Rock), from the [Mount of Olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_of_Olives)

Jerusalem is situated on the southern spur of a [plateau](538005.md) in the [Judaean Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaean_Mountains), which include the [Mount of Olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_of_Olives) (East) and [Mount Scopus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Scopus) (North East). The elevation of the Old City is approximately 760 m (2,490 ft).[^cite_note-193] The whole of Jerusalem is surrounded by valleys and dry [riverbeds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadi) (*wadis*). The [Kidron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidron_Valley), [Hinnom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gehenna), and [Tyropoeon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyropoeon_Valley) Valleys intersect in an area just south of the Old City of Jerusalem.[^cite_note-Bergsohn-2006-194] The Kidron Valley runs to the east of the Old City and separates the Mount of Olives from the city proper. Along the southern side of old Jerusalem is the Valley of Hinnom, a steep ravine associated in biblical [eschatology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eschatology) with the concept of [Gehenna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gehenna) or [Hell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hell).[^cite_note-195]

The [Tyropoeon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyropoeon_Valley) commenced in the northwest near the Damascus Gate, ran south-southeasterly through the centre of the Old City down to the [Pool of Siloam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pool_of_Siloam), and divided the lower part into two hills, the Temple Mount to the east, and the rest of the city to the west, the lower and the upper cities described by [Josephus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josephus). Today, this valley is hidden by debris that has accumulated over the centuries.[^cite_note-Bergsohn-2006-194] In biblical times Jerusalem was surrounded by forests of almond, olive and pine trees. These were destroyed by centuries of warfare and neglect. Dating back to the [Iron Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Israel#Early_Israelites_\(Iron_Age_I\)), farmers in the arid Jerusalem region built stone terraces along the slopes of hills to hold back the soil and allow for concentrated farming; these terraces are still very much in evidence in the Jerusalem landscape.[^cite_note-196]

Water supply has always been a major problem in Jerusalem, as attested to by the intricate network of ancient [aqueducts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aqueduct_\(watercourse\)), tunnels, pools and cisterns found in the city.[^cite_note-197]

Jerusalem is 60 km (37 mi)[^cite_note-198] east of [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md). On the opposite side of the city, approximately 35 km (22 mi)[^cite_note-199] away, is the [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea), the [lowest body of water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extremes_on_Earth) on Earth. Neighbouring cities and towns include [Bethlehem](4312.md) and [Beit Jala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Jala) to the south, [Abu Dis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dis) and [Ma'ale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Adumim) to the east, [Mevaseret Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mevaseret_Zion) to the west, and Ramallah and [Giv'at Ze'ev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giv'at_Ze'ev) to the north.[^cite_note-200][^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202]

[Mount Herzl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Herzl), at the western side of the city near the [Jerusalem Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Forest), serves as the national cemetery of Israel.[^cite_note-203] The body of [Theodor Herzl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodor_Herzl), the cemetery's namesake and the visionary behind the Zionist movement that led to the establishment of the State of Israel, was disinterred from a cemetery in [Vienna](55866.md), flown to Israel and buried at the site on August 17, 1949, before the formal opening of the cemetery three months later.[^cite_note-204]

### Climate



Snow visible on roofs in the Old City of Jerusalem

The city is characterised by a [hot-summer Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot-summer_Mediterranean_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *Csa*), with hot, dry summers, and mild, wet winters. Snow flurries usually occur once or twice a winter, although the city experiences heavy [snowfall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowfall) every three to four years, on average, with short-lived accumulation.

January is the coldest month of the year, with an average temperature of 9.1 °C (48.4 °F); July and August are the hottest months, with an average temperature of 24.2 °C (75.6 °F), and the summer months are usually rainless. The average annual precipitation is around 537 mm (21 in), with rain occurring almost entirely between October and May.[^cite_note-205] Snowfall is rare, and large snowfalls are even more rare.[^cite_note-Lappin-2013-206][^cite_note-Samenow-2013-207] Jerusalem received over 30 cm (12 in) of snow on 13 December 2013, which nearly paralysed the city.[^cite_note-Lappin-2013-206][^cite_note-Samenow-2013-207] A day in Jerusalem has on average, 9.3 sunshine hours. With summers averaging similar temperatures as the coastline, the maritime influence from the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md) is strong, in particular given that Jerusalem is located on a similar latitude as scorching hot deserts not far to its east.

The highest recorded temperature in Jerusalem was 44.4 °C (111.9 °F) on 28 and 30 August 1881, and the lowest temperature recorded was −6.7 °C (19.9 °F) on 25 January 1907.

Most of the air pollution in Jerusalem comes from vehicular traffic.[^cite_note-Ma'oz-2000-208] Many main streets in Jerusalem were not built to accommodate such a large volume of traffic, leading to traffic congestion and more [carbon monoxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_monoxide) released into the air. Industrial pollution inside the city is sparse, but emissions from factories on the [Israeli Mediterranean coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_coastal_plain) can travel eastward and settle over the city.[^cite_note-Ma'oz-2000-208][^cite_note-209]

## Demographics

### Demographic history

Main article: [Demographic history of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Jerusalem)

Jerusalem's population size and composition have shifted many times over its 5,000-year history. Since the 19th century, the [Old City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_City_\(Jerusalem\)) of Jerusalem has been divided into [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Quarter_\(Jerusalem\)), [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_Quarter_\(Jerusalem\)), [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Quarter) and [Armenian quarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Quarter). Matthew Teller writes that this convention may have originated in the [1841 British Royal Engineers map of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1840%E2%80%9341_Royal_Engineers_maps_of_Palestine,_Lebanon_and_Syria),[^cite_note-Teller-2022-21] or at least the Rev. [George Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Williams_\(priest\))' subsequent labelling of it.[^cite_note-215]

Most population data before 1905 are based on estimates, often from foreign travellers or organisations, since previous census data usually covered wider areas such as the [Jerusalem District](844333.md).[^cite_note-216] These estimates suggest that since the end of the [Crusades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusades), Muslims formed the largest group in Jerusalem until the mid-nineteenth century.

Between 1838 and 1876 a number of estimates exist which conflict as to whether Jews or Muslims were the largest group during this period, and between 1882 and 1922 estimates conflict as to exactly when Jews became an absolute majority of the population.

### Current demographics

See also: [Demographics of Jerusalem by quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Jerusalem_by_quarter)
